{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011LwMpTNya3KBZXbFMtRbhZ","type":"tool_result","content":"# Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n---\n## CI reported failures on 6c0a42c9960a0247e33b28157d3f80cae03e89ab\n\nThe build for your last push is red. These checks failed: eslint, test, spec-names, changelog\n\nWhere a failed step is named below, run only that step's command; otherwise map each name to the job that publishes it and run only that job's command.\n\n```\n### eslint (failure)\n\nsrc/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts\n   23:109  error  7 lines (58 tokens) duplicated with src/transport/sessionResume.test.ts:51-58 reported\n\n- Done: Validate a bare list_sessions frame (facet 2, bare-frame part)\n- Next: Validate limit and before on list_sessions in validation.ts\n---\n# Definition of Done\n\n> #143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)` inside the server process. The client on the other end of the WebSocket has no way to ask for it, and the consumer who wants a conversation picker without standing up their own REST endpoint has to write one anyway.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `createServer` and the WebSocket upgrade/message pipeline already exist and accept frames over a real WebSocket connection, so acceptance tests can connect today, send `list_sessions` frames, and fail directly on the missing `session_list` and `list_sessions` validation/dispatch behaviour.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId** — Sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit, before, userId}` over a connected WebSocket and verifies `store.listFor` is called with the socket's authenticated `userId` (`session.userId`) and `{limit, before}`, answering `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws** — Verifies that a store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}` and a store whose `listFor` throws/rejects logs an error under category `'ws'` with the userId and error message and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor** — Verifies that `{type: 'list_sessions'}` is accepted while non-integer or negative `limit` values and malformed `before` cursors (`sessionId` outside 1–128 chars or unparseable `updatedAt`) are rejected with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without invoking `store.listFor`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Add `ListSessionsMessage`, `SessionSummaryFrame`, and `SessionListMessage` to `src/types/messages.ts`, include them in `IncomingMessage` and `OutgoingMessage`, and re-export them from `src/index.ts`.\n- [x] Validate a bare `list_sessions` frame in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (returns `{type: 'list_sessions'}`; unknown fields such as `userId` are dropped). Round 2026-11-02: `ListSessionsMessage` added to `messages.ts` and `IncomingMessage`.\n- [ ] Validate `limit` and `before` on `list_sessions` in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (optional non-negative integer `limit`; optional `before` object with 1–128 char `sessionId` and finite-date `updatedAt` string or number; anything malformed is `INVALID_MESSAGE`).\n- [ ] Thread `sessionStore` into serialising `Date` fields to ISO strings, and on error logging via `log.error('ws', ...)` and sending `ErrorCodes.SERVER_ERROR`.\n- [ ] Document `list_sessions` and `session_list` in `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` and `specs/hal-engine-conversation-list/spec.md`.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Conversation titles or previews (first user message, model-generated title).\n- Deliver a user's conversation list over the WebSocket protocol\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#144\nLabels: type:feature, size:M, area:api, area:compliance, epic:extension-points\n\nPart of #35\n\n## Why\n\n#143 gives a consumer `store.listFor(userId)`.\nIt does not give a **client** anything: the browser holding the socket cannot reach the store, so\nevery consumer that wants a history sidebar has to stand up its own authenticated HTTP route over\nthe same data the engine already authenticated on the upgrade. That is a second authorisation\npath to the same personal data, written once per consumer, each one able to get the ownership\ncheck wrong in its own way — the exact failure #45 went out of its way to avoid for resume.\n\nA consumer may have no such route to extend: an engine deployment behind an api with a session\nstore and no conversation endpoints of its own has nothing to add this to. The engine already knows who is connected and already\nreplays a conversation on resume; answering \"which conversations do I have\" over the same socket\nis the smaller surface.\n\n## What to do\n\n- `src/types/messages.ts`, additive only:\n  - `ListConversationsMessage {type: 'list_conversations'; limit?: number; before?: string}` into\n    `IncomingMessage`. There is no user id field, and that is the point: the list is always the\n    connection's authenticated user's.\n  - `ConversationListMessage {type: 'conversation_list'; conversations: ConversationSummary[]}`\n    into `OutgoingMessage`, where `ConversationSummary` is `SessionSummary` with its two times as\n    ISO strings — `Date` does not survive `JSON.stringify` as a `Date`, and the protocol types\n    describe what goes on the wire.\n- `validation.ts`: a `list_conversations` case — `limit`, when present, an integer 1–200;\n  `before`, when present, a string `Date.parse` accepts. Anything else is `INVALID_MESSAGE`, as\n  the two existing cases do it.\n- Handling: `createMessageHandler` takes the session store and the new options alongside the\n  orchestrator (`createServer` already holds both), and answers `list_conversations` on the\n  non-locked path beside `ping` — a history request is not a turn and must not queue behind a\n  streaming answer.\n- The user id comes from `ws.userId`, never from the frame. Add the regression:\n  a frame carrying an extra `userId` of another user is answered with the connecting user's own\n  list.\n- Opt-in: `HalEngineConfig.transport.history?: {enabled: boolean}`, default off, threaded through\n  `createServer`'s options like `resume`. With it off, `list_conversations` is answered with an\n  `error` frame and no list.\n- A store that implements no `listFor` is answered with an `error`, not an empty list: a client\n  that renders \"no conversations\" for a user who has several is a worse answer than one that\n  renders a failure. `ErrorCodes` gains one code for both cases (`UNSUPPORTED`), additive.\n- Picking a conversation needs nothing new: the client reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`, which\n  #45 already ships with the ownership check and the replay. No in-band switch frame, no second\n  replay path.\n- Specs and docs: extend `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md` (the two frames, their\n  field tables, the authorisation rule) and the conversation-list spec; add § Conversation list\n  to `docs/getting-started.md` by editing `example/full-config.ts` and running `npm run docs:fix`\n  — that listing sits under `<!-- doc-block: example/full-config.ts#full-config -->` and\n  `npm run docs:check` fails a hand-edited block.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **A frame, not a REST route.** The REST chat routes are demo-only and are not where a consumer is meant to build. The WebSocket is where the caller is\n  already authenticated, once per connection, by the consumer's own `wsAuth`.\n- **Its own opt-in flag, separate from `resume`.** Resume hands a client back a conversation it\n  was already in; a list tells it what else the user has. A deployer who turned on resume did not\n  thereby agree to the second disclosure, and a config that conflates them cannot be audited.\n- **Answered off the per-session lock.** `PerSessionLock` serialises turns (#47). A list request\n  that queued behind a two-minute streaming answer would look broken, and it mutates nothing.\n- **An error, not an empty list, when it cannot be answered.** See above; silence that looks like\n  data is the one failure mode a history view cannot recover from.\n- **Answered when asked, never pushed.** No `conversation_list` arrives unsolicited: a client\n  that just started a conversation knows, and a push would need a fan-out across the sockets a\n  resumed session can be open on.\n- **The frame is accepted by `validateMessage` whether or not the feature is on**, and the\n  handler is what refuses it. Validation is static and has no config; the alternative is\n  threading options into a pure validator to turn a type into \"unknown\".\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `src/types/messages.ts` gains only new interfaces and their two union members (**amended:** four interfaces, not two — each frame needs a type for its own payload, `ConversationCursor` for what a client sends back and `ConversationSummary` for a row on the wire) — a\n      `git diff` of that file touches no existing line, and a fixture pins that every message\n      literal valid before this change still compiles\n- [ ] `validateMessage({type: 'list_conversations'})` is valid; with `limit: 0`, `limit: 201`,\n      `limit: 1.5`, `limit: '10'` or `before: 'not a date'` it is invalid with a message naming\n      the field\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` and a store holding three conversations for the user, a\n      `list_conversations` frame is answered with exactly one `conversation_list` carrying three\n      summaries, newest first, each `createdAt` and `updatedAt` an ISO string, and no\n      `stream_end`\n- [ ] A frame carrying `{type: 'list_conversations', userId: '<another user>'}` is answered with\n      the connecting user's own list, and the store's `listFor` is called with `ws.userId` —\n      asserted on the recorded argument, not only on the frame\n- [ ] `limit` and `before` reach `listFor` as the frame sent them. **Amended:** `before` is a\n      `{updatedAt, sessionId}` cursor of ISO strings, not a `Date`. This was written before\n      #143's review found that paging on a timestamp alone drops a conversation whenever two\n      share an activity time, so the store's cursor became a composite that accepts an ISO\n      string; a `Date` cannot carry the tie-break. The handler passes `{limit, before}` with\n      both possibly `undefined` rather than omitting the argument, which is the same call\n- [ ] With `history` absent, the same frame is answered with one `error` frame whose code is\n      `UNSUPPORTED` and no `conversation_list`\n- [ ] With `history: {enabled: true}` against a store implementing no `listFor`, the answer is\n      the same `error` frame, and `grep` shows no code path that sends `conversations: []`\n- [ ] A `listFor` that rejects is answered with one `error` frame whose code is `SERVER_ERROR`\n      and whose message carries no store detail, the socket stays open, and the failure is\n      logged with the session id and no user content. **Added during the build:** a store throwing\n      the new exported `StoreCannotList` is answered `UNSUPPORTED` instead, so an injected\n      collection without a `find` reads as the misconfiguration it is rather than a database\n      that failed\n- [ ] A `list_conversations` sent while a `user_message` is streaming on the same session is\n      answered before that turn's `stream_end` — it does not wait on the lock\n- [ ] A client that takes an id from a `conversation_list` and reconnects with `?sessionId=<id>`\n      receives `resumed: true` and that conversation's replay, with no new transport code: the\n      test exercises the existing #45 path end to end\n- [ ] `ConnectedMessage` is unchanged by this issue\n- [ ] **GDPR.** The protocol spec states that `conversation_list` carries one authenticated\n      user's own conversation metadata and no conversation content, that the user id is taken\n      from the connection and never from the frame, and that `transport.history` is the\n      deployer's decision to make a user's history reachable from the client; `SessionResumeOptions`'\n      retention note is mirrored on the new options type, pointing at #41\n- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` carries an entry under `## [Unreleased]` written for somebody installing the\n      package\n- [ ] The `docs/getting-started.md` change is made in `example/full-config.ts` and the doc\n      regenerated with `npm run docs:fix`, not typed into the markdown\n- [ ] Spec statements carry trailing `([validated by: ...](...#Lnn))` citations, `Status` rows\n      match coverage, and `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n      `npm run spec:names` pass\n- [ ] The version bump is MINOR — new optional config, two additive protocol frames, one new\n      error code — and the PR body says so; no migration guide is owed\n- [ ] `npm run verify` passes and the coverage floors for the touched groups hold or rise\n\n## Amendments\n\nThree things changed between writing this issue and building it, each marked in the criterion it\naffects:\n\n1. **`before` is a cursor, not a timestamp.** #143's review found that paging on `updatedAt` alone\n   silently drops a conversation when two share an activity time. The store's cursor became\n   `{updatedAt, sessionId}`, so the frame carries both halves and the validator refuses one half\n   alone. Built as originally written, the transport would have reintroduced the defect the store\n   had just been fixed for.\n2. **`messages.ts` gains four interfaces, not two** — the two frames plus the two payload types\n   they describe.\n3. **`StoreCannotList` is new public surface**, beyond what this issue specified: without it a\n   permanent store misconfiguration cannot be told from a transient failure, in either the client\n   frame or the log.\n\nTwo decisions the criteria did not anticipate:\n\n- **`transport.history` without `transport.resume` is useless, and now says so.** `?sessionId=` is\n  read only when resume is enabled, so a list whose rows cannot be opened is a deployment slip:\n  the engine warns at startup and the documentation says so where it tells a client to reconnect\n  with a row's id. Refusing to start was considered and rejected as too blunt for a library.\n- **The conversation the connection is in is listed like any other**, so a fresh connect sees a row\n  of its own holding no entries. Filtering it out was rejected: it is a real conversation the user\n  is in, and it is their newest the moment they send anything.\n\n## Tech notes\n\nDepends on #143: without `listFor` there is\nnothing to send.\n\nKey paths: `src/types/messages.ts`, `src/types/session.ts` (`ErrorCodes`),\n`src/transport/ws/{validation,messageHandler,sender}.ts`, `src/transport/createServer.ts`,\n`src/config.ts`, `example/full-config.ts`, `specs/hal-engine-websocket-protocol/spec.md`.\n\nOut of scope: a preview or title on the summaries (its own issue); deleting a conversation from\nthe client, which would put an erasure primitive on the wire and needs its own authorisation\ndecision; paging state held server-side — the client pages with `before`; an operator view of\nanother user's conversations, which is an authorisation question of its own; and live sync between open tabs,\nwhich #124 ruled out.\n\nRefs #143, #145, #41, #45, #47, #124\n\n\n\n\n---\n## CI reported failures on 6c0a42c9960a0247e33b28157d3f80cae03e89ab\n\nThe build for your last push is red. These checks failed: eslint, test, spec-names, changelog\n\nWhere a failed step is named below, run only that step's command; otherwise map each name to the job that publishes it and run only that consumer `store.listFor(userId)` inside the server process. The client on the other end of the WebSocket has no way to ask for it, and the consumer who wants a conversation picker without standing up their own REST endpoint has to write one anyway.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `createServer` and the WebSocket upgrade/message pipeline already exist and accept frames over a real WebSocket connection, so acceptance tests can connect today, send `list_sessions` frames, and fail directly on the missing `session_list` and `list_sessions` validation/dispatch behaviour.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId** — Sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit, before, userId}` over a connected WebSocket and verifies `store.listFor` is called with the socket's authenticated `userId` (`session.userId`) and `{limit, before}`, answering `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws** — Verifies that a store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}` and a store whose `listFor` throws/rejects logs an error under category `'ws'` with the userId and error message and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n- [ ] **listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol › accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor** — Verifies that `{type: 'list_sessions'}` is accepted while non-integer or negative `limit` values and malformed `before` cursors (`sessionId` outside 1–128 chars or unparseable `updatedAt`) are rejected with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without invoking `store.listFor`.\n  `src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Add `ListSessionsMessage`, `SessionSummaryFrame`, and `SessionListMessage` to `src/types/messages.ts`, include them in `IncomingMessage` and `OutgoingMessage`, and re-export them from `src/index.ts`.\n- Round 2026-11-02: `ListSessionsMessage` added to `messages.ts` and `IncomingMessage`.\n- [ ] Validate `limit` and `before` on `list_sessions` in `src/transport/ws/validation.ts` (optional non-negative integer `limit`; Jest has no dry-run, so the list command executes the suite\n    once with --json and derives every name from the report; it deliberately\n    tolerates a red suite, since listing must not depend on the tests passing.\n    The run command selects one test with --runTestsByPath plus an anchored,\n    regex-escaped -t, and treats a selector that matches nothing as a failure\n    rather than a green run of an empty suite. Neither command builds first:\n    no test here reads dist/.\n  list: node scripts/lore-list-tests.mjs\n  run: node scripts/lore-run-test.mjs \"{selector}\"\n  coverage_format: from '../types/sessionStore.js';\n9\timport {setLogger} from '../shared/logger.js';\n10\t\n11\t// Real upgrades over a real socket: what the list_sessions frame answers is the contract, and no double can observe it.\n12\t\n13\tinterface Frame {\n14\t  type: string;\n15\t  sessionId?: string;\n16\t  sessions?: Array<{sessionId: string; createdAt: string; updatedAt: string; entryCount: number}>;\n17\t  code?: string;\n18\t  message?: string;\n19\t}\n20\t\n21\tconst servers: Array<ReturnType<typeof createServer>> = [];\n22\tconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\n23\tconst logLines: Array<{level: string; category: string; message: string; fields?: Record<string, unknown>}> = [];\n24\t\n25\tconst eventually = async (check: () => boolean): Promise<void> => {\n26\t  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 50 && !check(); attempt++) await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 10));\n27\t};\n28\t\n29\tbeforeEach(() => {\n30\t  logLines.length = 0;\n31\t  const record = (level: string) => (category: string, message: string, fields?: Record<string, unknown>) =>\n32\t    void logLines.push({level, category, message, fields});\n33\t  setLogger({debug: record('debug'), info: record('info'), warn: record('warn'), error: record('error')});\n34\t});\n35\t\n36\tafterEach(async () => {\n37\t  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n38\t  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {\n39\t    const {wss} = hal;\n40\t    await eventually(() => wss.clients.size === 0);\n41\t    await hal.stop();\n42\t  }\n43\t  setLogger();\n44\t});\n45\t\n46\tconst startEngine = async (store: SessionStore, userId: string | number = 'u1') => {\n47\t  const hal = createServer({\n48\t    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n49\t    wsAuth: async () => ({id: userId}),\n50\t    sessionStore: store,\n51\t    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n52\t  });\n53\t  await hal.start(0);\n54\t  servers.push(hal);\n55\t  const {port} = hal.server.address() as const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, 't']);\n61\t  clients.push(client);\n62\t  const frames: Frame[] = [];\n63\t  let connected: Frame = {type: ''};\n64\t  await new Promise<void>(resolve => {\n65\t    client.on('message', raw => {\n66\t      const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as Frame;\n67\t      if (frame.type === 'connected') {\n68\t        connected = frame;\n69\t        client.send(typeof outbound === 'string' ? outbound : JSON.stringify(outbound));\n70\t        client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'ping', timestamp: 1}));\n71\t        return;\n72\t      }\n73\t      if (frame.type === 'pong') {\n74\t        resolve();\n75\t        return;\n76\t      }\n77\t      frames.push(frame);\n78\t    });\n79\t  });\n80\t  return {connected,  count: () => inner.count(),\n89\t  clear: () => inner.clear(),\n90\t});\n91\t\n92\tdescribe('listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol', () => {\n93\t  it('answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId', async () => {\n94\t    const inner = new InMemorySessionStore();\n95\t    const seen: Array<{userId: string | number; options?: SessionListOptions}> = [];\n96\t    const summaries: SessionSummary[] = [\n97\t      {\n98\t        sessionId: 's2',\n99\t        createdAt: new Date('2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z'),\n100\t        updatedAt: new Date('2026-01-01T00:02:00.000Z'),\n101\t        entryCount: 4,\n102\t      },\n103\t    ];\n104\t    const store: SessionStore = {\n105\t      ...delegatingTo(inner),\n106\t      listFor: (userId, options) => {\n107\t        seen.push({userId, options});\n108\t        return summaries;\n109\t      },\n110\t    };\n111\t    const url = await startEngine(store, 'u1');\n112\t    const before: SessionCursor = {updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:05:00.000Z', sessionId: 's3'};\n113\t\n114\t    const {frames} = await askAfterConnect(url, {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 10, before, userId: 'u2'});\n115\t\n116\t    expect({seen, frames}).toEqual({\n117\t      seen: [{userId: 'u1', options: {limit: 10, before}}],\n118\t      frames: [\n119\t        {\n120\t          type: 'session_list',\n121\t          sessions: [\n122\t            {\n123\t              sessionId: 's2',\n124\t              createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',\n125\t              updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:02:00.000Z',\n126\t              entryCount: 4,\n127\t            },\n128\t          ],\n129\t        },\n130\t      ],\n131\t    });\n132\t  });\n133\t\n134\t  it('answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws', async () => {\n135\t    const inner = new InMemorySessionStore();\n136\t    const withoutList = await startEngine(delegatingTo(inner));\n137\t    const failingList = await startEngine({\n138\t      ...delegatingTo(inner),\n139\t      listFor: () => Promise.reject(new Error('database offline')),\n140\t    });\n141\t\n142\t    const unimplemented = await askAfterConnect(withoutList, {type: 'list_sessions'});\n143\t    const failed = await askAfterConnect(failingList, {type: 'list_sessions'});\n144\t\n145\t    expect({\n146\t      unimplemented: unimplemented.frames,\n147\t      failed: failed.frames.map(frame => ({type: frame.type, code: frame.code})),\n148\t      logged: logLines.filter(line => line.level === 'error' && line.category === 'ws'),\n149\t    }).toEqual({\n150\t      unimplemented: [{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}],\n151\t      failed: [{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}],\n152\t      logged: [\n153\t        {\n154\t          level: 'error',\n155\t          category: 'ws',\n156\t          message: expect.any(String),\n157\t          fields: expect.objectContaining({userId: 'u1', error: 'database offline'}),\n158\t        },\n159\t      ],\n160\t    });\n161\t  });\n162\t\n163\t  it('accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor', async () => {\n164\t    const inner = new InMemorySessionStore();\n165\t    let calls = 0;\n166\t    const store: SessionStore = {\n167\t      ...delegatingTo(inner),\n168\t      listFor: () => {\n169\t        calls += 1;\n170\t        return [];\n171\t      },\n172\t    };\n173\t    const url = await startEngine(store);\n174\t    const badFrames = [\n175\t      {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 'ten'},\n176\t      {type: 'list_sessions', limit: -1},\n177\t      {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 1.5},\n178\t      {type: 'list_sessions', before: 'not-an-object'},\n179\t      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z', sessionId: ''}},\n180\t      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z', sessionId: 'a'.repeat(129)}},\n181\t      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: 'not-a-date', sessionId: 's1'}},\n182\t      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: true, sessionId: 's1'}},\n183\t    ];\n184\t\n185\t    const codes: Array<string | undefined> = [];\n186\t    for (const bad of type {AddressInfo} from 'node:net';\nimport {WebSocket} from 'ws';\nimport type {Express} from 'express';\nimport {createServer} from './createServer.js';\nimport {HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL} from './ws/subprotocol.js';\nimport {answeringOrchestrator} from './wsTestSupport.js';\nimport {InMemorySessionStore} from '../infrastructure/stores/inMemorySessionStore.js';\nimport type {SessionCursor, SessionListOptions, SessionStore, SessionSummary} from '../types/sessionStore.js';\nimport {setLogger} from '../shared/logger.js';\n\n// Real upgrades over a real socket: what the list_sessions frame answers is the contract, and no double can observe it.\n\ninterface Frame {\n  type: string;\n  sessionId?: string;\n  sessions?: Array<{sessionId: string; createdAt: string; updatedAt: string; entryCount: number}>;\n  code?: string;\n  message?: string;\n}\n\nconst servers: Array<ReturnType<typeof createServer>> = [];\nconst clients: WebSocket[] = [];\nconst logLines: Array<{level: string; Record<string, unknown>) =>\n    void logLines.push({level, category, message, fields});\n  setLogger({debug: record('debug'), info: record('info'), warn: record('warn'), error: record('error')});\n});\n\nafterEach(async () => {\n  for (const client of clients.splice(0)) client.terminate();\n  for (const hal of servers.splice(0)) {\n    const {wss} = hal;\n    await eventually(() => wss.clients.size === 0);\n    await hal.stop();\n  }\n  setLogger();\n});\n\nconst startEngine = async (store: SessionStore, userId: string | number = 'u1') => {\n  const hal = createServer({\n    app: (() => undefined) as unknown as Express,\n    wsAuth: async () => ({id: userId}),\n    sessionStore: store,\n    orchestrator: answeringOrchestrator,\n  });\n  await hal.start(0);\n  servers.push(hal);\n  const {port} = hal.server.address() as AddressInfo;\n  return `ws://127.0.0.1:${port}/hal/ws`;\n};\n\nconst askAfterConnect = async (url: string, outbound: unknown): Promise<{connected: Frame; frames: Frame[]}> => {\n  const client = new WebSocket(url, [HAL_WS_SUBPROTOCOL, 't']);\n  clients.push(client);\n  const frames: Frame[] = [];\n  let connected: Frame = {type: ''};\n  await new Promise<void>(resolve => {\n    client.on('message', raw => {\n      const frame = JSON.parse(String(raw)) as Frame;\n      if (frame.type === 'connected') {\n        connected = frame;\n        client.send(typeof outbound === 'string' ? outbound : JSON.stringify(outbound));\n        client.send(JSON.stringify({type: 'ping', timestamp: 1}));\n        return;\n      }\n      if (frame.type === 'pong') {\n        resolve();\n        return;\n      }\n      frames.push(frame);\n    });\n  });\n  return {connected, frames};\n};\n\n// A store with only the five required members, so listFor can be omitted, recorded or made to throw.\nconst delegatingTo = (inner: InMemorySessionStore): SessionStore => ({\n  create: (sessionId, userId, options) => inner.create(sessionId, userId, options),\n  get: sessionId => inner.get(sessionId),\n  delete: sessionId => inner.delete(sessionId),\n  count: () => inner.count(),\n  clear: () => inner.clear(),\n});\n\ndescribe('listing conversations over the WebSocket protocol', () => {\n  it('answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId', async () => {\n    const inner = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    const seen: Array<{userId: string | number; options?: SessionListOptions}> = [];\n    const summaries: SessionSummary[] = [\n      {\n        sessionId: 's2',\n        createdAt: new Date('2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z'),\n        updatedAt: new Date('2026-01-01T00:02:00.000Z'),\n        entryCount: 4,\n      },\n    ];\n    const store: SessionStore = {\n      ...delegatingTo(inner),\n      listFor: (userId, options) => {\n        seen.push({userId, options});\n        return summaries;\n      },\n    };\n    const url = await startEngine(store, 'u1');\n    const before: SessionCursor = {updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:05:00.000Z', sessionId: 's3'};\n\n    const {frames} = await askAfterConnect(url, {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 10, before, userId: 'u2'});\n\n    expect({seen, frames}).toEqual({\n      seen: [{userId: 'u1', options: {limit: 10, before}}],\n      frames: [\n        {\n          type: 'session_list',\n          sessions: [\n            {\n              sessionId: 's2',\n              createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',\n              updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:02:00.000Z',\n              entryCount: 4,\n            },\n          ],\n        },\n      ],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws', async () => {\n    const inner = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    const withoutList = await startEngine(delegatingTo(inner));\n    const failingList = await startEngine({\n      ...delegatingTo(inner),\n      listFor: () => Promise.reject(new Error('database offline')),\n    });\n\n    const unimplemented = await askAfterConnect(withoutList, {type: 'list_sessions'});\n    const failed = await askAfterConnect(failingList, {type: 'list_sessions'});\n\n    expect({\n      unimplemented: unimplemented.frames,\n      failed: failed.frames.map(frame => ({type: frame.type, code: frame.code})),\n      logged: logLines.filter(line => line.level === 'error' && line.category === 'ws'),\n    }).toEqual({\n      unimplemented: [{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}],\n      failed: [{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}],\n      logged: [\n        {\n          level: 'error',\n          category: 'ws',\n          message: expect.any(String),\n          fields: expect.objectContaining({userId: 'u1', error: 'database offline'}),\n        },\n      ],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it('accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor', async () => {\n    const inner = new InMemorySessionStore();\n    let calls = 0;\n    const store: SessionStore = {\n      ...delegatingTo(inner),\n      listFor: () => {\n        calls += 1;\n        return [];\n      },\n    };\n    const url = await startEngine(store);\n    const badFrames = [\n      {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 'ten'},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', limit: -1},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', limit: 1.5},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: 'not-an-object'},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z', sessionId: ''}},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z', sessionId: 'a'.repeat(129)}},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: 'not-a-date', sessionId: 's1'}},\n      {type: 'list_sessions', before: {updatedAt: true, sessionId: 's1'}},\n    ];\n\n    const codes: Array<string | undefined> = [];\n    for (const bad of badFrames) {\n      const {frames} = await askAfterConnect(url, bad);\n      codes.push(frames[0]?.code);\n    }\n    const {frames: accepted} = await askAfterConnect(url, {type: 'list_sessions'});\n\n    expect({codes, accepted, calls}).toEqual({\n      codes: Array(badFrames.length).fill('INVALID_MESSAGE'),\n      accepted: [{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}],\n      calls: 1,\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":199,"startLine":1,"totalLines":199}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01Dhm4DdedWvZwRehyAaAz3H","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QhbrSMzFuri68QhG1pXZ6b","type":"tool_result","content":"# Conversation List\n\n| Field  | Value                 |\n| ------ | --------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#143 |\n| Status | Shipped               |\n\nResume gets a client back into one conversation: the id it names, or the user's most recently active one. Neither tells it which conversations exist, and every other member of `SessionStore` answers about a session the caller can already name, so a consumer wanting to offer a conversation list had to query the collection behind the engine's back, against a document shape and a key the engine owns. `listFor` answers the list instead, as summaries rather than sessions. **GDPR.** A summary is personal data — that a user held a conversation, and when — so it carries no conversation content, it is filtered to one user by the query rather than in application code afterwards, and the retention bound stays the consumer's, through the store's `maxAgeMs` and erasure methods and re-cinq/HALEngine#41.\n\n## The summary\n\n- A summary is `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount`, and nothing else: neither the conversation's entries nor its credentials, so a list can never hand over either, even for a session created with credentials ([validated by: carries only its four fields, so a list can never hand over a conversation or a credential](../../src/infrastructure/stores/sessionStoreListSignature.test.ts#L22)).\n- `listFor` is optional, like `latestFor` and `lookup` before it: a store written against the five original members still satisfies the interface, and both shipped stores implement it ([validated by: leaves a store with only the five original members satisfying the interface beside both shipped ones](../../src/infrastructure/stores/sessionStoreListSignature.test.ts#L11)).\n\n## The order a list answers in\n\n- Summaries come back most recent activity first ([validated by: answers newest activity first, headed by the very session latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L27)).\n- The first summary is the session `latestFor` returns, because both read the same activity ([validated by: answers newest activity first, headed by the very session latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L27)).\n- The order is total rather than merely sorted: two conversations last active in the same millisecond are ordered by session id, the larger first, which is what lets a page resume without dropping one of them ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on activity rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L141)).\n- In `InMemorySessionStore` that tie-break is the list's alone: `latestFor` breaks the same tie by creation order, so for two tied conversations the head of the list and the session a resume rejoins can differ ([validated by: breaks a tie on activity by session id, where latestFor takes the session created later](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L42)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` answers in order of `updatedAt`, which each `save` moves forward, and its first summary is the session its own `latestFor` returns ([validated by: answers the user's conversations newest saved first, headed by the one latestFor returns](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L31)).\n- It breaks a tie by `_id` in both members, so there the head of the list is the session a resume rejoins even when every conversation shares an `updatedAt` ([validated by: heads the list with the session latestFor returns even when every conversation shares an updatedAt](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L161)).\n\n## What a summary reports\n\n- `InMemorySessionStore` reports the moment `create` was called, the newest entry time as the activity, and the conversation's entry count ([validated by: reports a creation time, the newest activity and the entry count of each conversation](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L53)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` reports the document's `createdAt`, the `updatedAt` its last save wrote, and the counter written beside the entries ([validated by: reports the creation time, the last save and the entry count of each conversation](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L49)).\n- That counter is what a save writes, so a list never reads a conversation to count one ([validated by: counts a conversation from the field a save writes beside the entries](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L95)).\n- A document written before the counter existed reads as a conversation of no entries rather than failing the list, which is what the backlog documented in `docs/session-stores.md` is for ([validated by: reads a conversation saved before the counter existed as one of no entries, rather than failing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L79)).\n\n## Whose conversations\n\n- A list holds only the asking user's conversations: another user's never appear, and a user with none gets an empty list ([validated by: omits a conversation that aged out and another user's, and answers nothing for a user with none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L65)).\n- `InMemorySessionStore` omits a conversation that has aged out, as `latestFor` does ([validated by: omits a conversation that aged out and another user's, and answers nothing for a user with none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L65)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` filters on the user in the database, sorts on `{updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}` — the `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1}` index `latestFor` already needs serves its leading keys — and asks for a projection that leaves the entries behind ([validated by: asks the database to filter on the user, sort on the index with the id behind it, and never for the entries](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L108)).\n- It answers from that projection alone, so a collection that never returns an entry still lists conversations — the conversation does not leave the database to be listed ([validated by: answers from a projection alone, so a collection that never returns entries still lists conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L181)).\n\n## Paging\n\n- `limit` takes that many of the newest, and `before` resumes after a row, so a client pages by passing back the last summary it saw — `SessionCursor` is `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, which a `SessionSummary` already satisfies ([validated by: pages down the same order: a limit takes the newest, and before takes what is older than it](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L78)).\n- Paging walks every conversation exactly once even when all of them share an activity time, because the cursor carries the session id the order breaks ties on: a tie cannot fall between two pages and be skipped ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on activity rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L141)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` pages the same way ([validated by: pages with before and a limit, down the same newest-first order](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L62)).\n- It walks a tie exactly once too, through the same cursor ([validated by: keeps a conversation tied on updatedAt rather than dropping it across a page boundary](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L127)).\n- A list is at most 200 summaries however many are asked for, and 50 when none is asked for, so no caller can ask for an unbounded page ([validated by: answers at most two hundred conversations however many are asked for, and fifty when none is](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L94)).\n- That limit bounds the rows answered, not the rows the database reads to answer them: on `MongoSessionStore` the work is bounded by the `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}` index `docs/session-stores.md` prescribes, and under the two-key form Mongo sorts the user's whole history before applying the limit ([validated by: needs the three-key index to answer without sorting the whole history, which the two-key form does not](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L192)).\n- A fractional limit is floored rather than refused, since a slip in a limit is not a reason to deny a user their history ([validated by: floors a fractional limit, and reads a negative or unusable one as none given rather than as nothing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L104)).\n- A negative limit, and one that is not a usable number, are read as no limit given at all rather than as a limit of none: handing a user an empty history for a typo is the worse reading ([validated by: floors a fractional limit, and reads a negative or unusable one as none given rather than as nothing](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L104)).\n- Only a limit of exactly `0` answers no conversations ([validated by: answers nothing only for a limit of exactly none](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L116)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` answers a limit of `0` without querying, because MongoDB reads `limit: 0` as no limit at all and would otherwise answer with every conversation the filter matches ([validated by: answers a limit of none without asking the database, which reads a limit of zero as no limit at all](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L143)).\n\n## The two shipped stores answer one order\n\n- `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` list the same conversations in the same order, so a consumer swapping one for the other sees the same conversation list ([validated by: answer the same order for conversations whose activity differs](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L43)).\n- They break a tie on activity the same way, by session id ([validated by: break a tie on activity the same way, by session id](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L52)).\n- They page identically through the documented call, `listFor(userId, {limit, before: rows.at(-1)})`, a page at a time until a page comes back empty ([validated by: page the same way through the documented call, two rows at a time from the last row seen](../../src/infrastructure/stores/storeListAgreement.test.ts#L61)).\n\n## A cursor the caller can actually hold\n\n- A cursor's `updatedAt` may be a `Date`, an ISO string or an epoch number, so a summary that went through JSON on its way to a client and back still pages ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, and refuses one carrying no usable moment](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L123)).\n- `MongoSessionStore` reads such a cursor as a date before it queries, since a string compares against no stored BSON date and would quietly match none of them ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, where a string would have matched no stored date](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L150)).\n- A cursor carrying no usable moment is refused with an error naming it, rather than read as no cursor at all, which would answer the first page again and repeat rows a client has already shown ([validated by: pages on a cursor that has been through JSON, and refuses one carrying no usable moment](../../src/infrastructure/stores/inMemoryConversationList.test.ts#L123)).\n\n## A collection that cannot list\n\n- `find` is optional on `CollectionLike`, so an adapter written before conversation lists existed still satisfies the port ([validated by: refuses to list on a collection that implements no find, rather than reporting no conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L175)).\n- A store built on a collection without it refuses to list, rather than reporting that the user has no conversations ([validated by: refuses to list on a collection that implements no find, rather than reporting no conversations](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L175)).\n\n## When the store cannot answer\n\n- A store whose collection fails rejects rather than answering a partial or empty list: what to show a user who may have conversations is the caller's decision, and an empty history is not a safe guess ([validated by: rejects rather than answering a partial list when the collection fails](../../src/infrastructure/stores/mongo/mongoConversationList.test.ts#L225)).\n\n## Over the WebSocket protocol\n\n- A client sends `{type: 'list_sessions', limit?, before?}` and receives `{type: 'session_list', sessions}` for the socket's authenticated user, with `createdAt` and `updatedAt` serialised as ISO-8601 strings and any client-supplied `userId` ignored ([validated by: answers list_sessions for the authenticated user with ISO dates, forwarding limit and before while ignoring any client userId](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L87)).\n- A store without `listFor` answers `{type: 'session_list', sessions: []}`, and a throwing `listFor` logs an error on the `ws` category and answers `{type: 'error', code: 'SERVER_ERROR'}` ([validated by: answers an empty session_list when the store has no listFor, and SERVER_ERROR with a ws error log when listFor throws](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L129)).\n- The validator accepts an optional non-negative integer `limit` and an optional `before` cursor whose `sessionId` is 1–128 characters and whose `updatedAt` parses as a valid timestamp, rejecting any other `list_sessions` payload with `INVALID_MESSAGE` without calling `listFor` ([validated by: accepts a bare list_sessions frame and rejects a malformed limit or before with INVALID_MESSAGE without calling listFor](../../src/transport/conversationList.acceptance.test.ts#L161)).\n----\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore.listFor(userId, options?)`, an optional store member that answers the summaries of a user's conversations — `sessionId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` and `entryCount` — most recent activity first, so a consumer can build a conversation list instead of only rejoining one. Nothing in the engine calls it yet: the store answers the list, and the route or frame that carries it to a client is still yours to write. `InMemorySessionStore` and `MongoSessionStore` both implement it; a store of your own may leave it out. A summary never carries a conversation's entries or its credentials. Page with `limit` (50 by default, 200 at most, and `0` answers nothing) and `before`, which takes the last summary you saw: the cursor carries both `updatedAt` and `sessionId`, so two conversations saved in the same millisecond cannot straddle a page boundary and go unlisted. See `docs/session-stores.md` § Listing a user's conversations.\n\n### Changed\n\n- `MongoSessionStore` now writes an `entryCount` field beside the entries on every save, so a conversation list can count a conversation without reading one. A document saved by an earlier version lists as a conversation of no entries until you backfill it; `docs/session-stores.md` carries the one-line command.\n- `MongoSessionStore.latestFor` now breaks a tie on `updatedAt` by `_id`, so it and `listFor` agree on which conversation is newest when two were saved in the same millisecond. Which of two tied conversations a resume rejoins can therefore change.\n- The index to create for `MongoSessionStore` is now `{userId: 1, updatedAt: -1, _id: -1}`. The two-key form that served `latestFor` cannot satisfy `listFor`'s sort, so Mongo would sort a user's whole history on every page. `docs/session-stores.md` § Latest session has the command.\n- `CollectionLike` gains an optional `find`, and `CursorLike`, `FindManyOptions` and `FindOneOptions` are now exported for anyone writing their own adapter. A collection without `find` still satisfies the type; `listFor` refuses on it rather than reporting that the user has no conversations.\n\n## [0.5.0] - 2026-10-04\n\n**Upgrading from 0.4.x.** One thing is removed, and several things an existing consumer observes change. Check these before you bump:\n\n- Every client must offer `hal.v1` beside its token, `new WebSocket(url, ['hal.v1', token])`. A bare-token offer, accepted with a deprecation warning since 0.3.0, now fails to connect, and `credentialFromSubprotocol` returns `undefined` for it.\n- Closing a socket abandons its turn: the run stops reading from the model and abandons its tool calls, no `stream_end` follows the close, and `afterModelResponse` and `onError` do not fire for it, while `afterSession` still does. A missed heartbeat and `stop()` close sockets too, so drain connections before a deploy.\n- Every tool call now has 30 seconds to settle, a `beforeToolCall` policy included. A tool that legitimately runs longer needs a larger `toolTimeoutMs`, or `0` for the old unbounded wait.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` no longer throws for a tool name the model invented, or rejects when an executor throws: the model is answered instead. It throws a `TypeError` for an input that is not an object, whatever the tool's schema.\n- Messages in one session are answered one at a time, so a message sent while another is streaming starts after that one's `stream_end`.\n----\n#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Holds every `([validated by: <test name>](path#Lnn))` citation to the test it names.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs           # report, exit 1 on any finding\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs --fix     # repoint each line from the name it carries\n//\n// A line number is the wrong thing to cite: inserting an import above a suite moves every test below\n// it, and the citation silently lands on a different, still-valid declaration. The name is what the\n// author meant, so the name is what the citation records and the line becomes a derived pointer this\n// script repairs. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job both index by it, and a\n// citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than one test.\nimport {readFileSync, writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport process from 'node:process';\nimport {dirname, join, normalize} from 'node:path';\nimport {root} from './lib/repo-root.mjs';\n\nconst CITATION = /\\[validated by(?<label>: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>(?:\\.\\.\\/)+[\\w./-]+?)#L(?<line>\\d+)\\)/g;\n// Any other href shape is refused rather than ignored: the coverage job resolves `./../x` and `/x`\n// to the same file and counts them, while this gate and the drift check would never have read them.\nconst ANY_HREF = /\\[validated by(?:: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>[^)#\\s]+)#L\\d+\\)/g;\n// `xit`, `xdescribe` and `.skip` declare a test that never runs, which validates nothing.\nconst DECLARATION = /^\\s*(?:f?it|f?describe|test)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst SKIPPED = /^\\s*(?:x(?:it|describe|test)|(?:it|describe|test)\\.skip)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(?:test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\n\nconst cache = new Map();\nconst args = process.argv.slice(2);\nconst fix = args.includes('--fix');\nconst named = args.filter(arg => !arg.startsWith('--'));\nconst findings = [];\nlet checked = 0;\nlet repaired = 0;\n\nfor (const spec of named.length > 0 ? named : specs()) {\n  const source = readFileSync(join(root, spec), 'utf8');\n  const specDir = dirname(spec);\n  let changed = false;\n\n  for (const match of source.matchAll(ANY_HREF)) {\n    if (!match.groups.path.startsWith('../')) {\n      findings.push(`${spec}: href \"${match.groups.path}\" must be relative and start with ../`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  const rewritten = source.replace(CITATION, (whole, label, path, line, ...rest) => {\n    const groups = rest[rest.length - 1];\n    const target = normalize(join(specDir, groups.path));\n    if (!TEST_FILE.test(target)) return whole;\n\n    checked += 1;\n    const declarations = declarationsIn(target);\n    if (declarations === null) {\n      findings.push(`${spec}: ${groups.path} does not exist`);\n      return whole;\n    }\n\n    const named = groups.label ? groups.label.slice(2) : null;\n    const atLine = declarations.find(declaration => declaration.line === Number(groups.line));\n\n    const skipped = declarations.find(d => d.skipped && (d.name === named || d.line === Number(groups.line)));\n    if (skipped) {\n      findings.push(`${spec}: cites \"${skipped.name}\" at ${groups.path}#L${skipped.line}, which is skipped and validates nothing`);\n      return whole;\n    }\n\n    if (named === null) {\n      if (!atLine) {\n        findings.push(`${spec}: ${groups.path}#L${groups.line} is not a declaration, so no name can be read from it`);\n        return whole;\n      }\n      if (nameIsAmbiguous(declarations, atLine.name)) {\n        findings.push(ambiguity(spec, groups.path, atLine.name, declarations));\n        return whole;\n      }\n      if (!fix) {\n        findings.push(`${spec}: ${groups.path}#L${groups.line} carries no test name`);\n        return whole;\n      }\n----\nspec.md\nimport type {IncomingMessage} from '../../types/messages.js';\n\nconst MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH = 10000;\n\ninterface ValidationSuccess {\n  valid: true;\n  data: IncomingMessage;\n}\n\ninterface ValidationFailure {\n  valid: false;\n  error: string;\n}\n\nexport type ValidationResult = ValidationSuccess | ValidationFailure;\n\nexport function validateMessage(payload: unknown): ValidationResult {\n  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must be an object'};\n  }\n\n  const message = payload as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n  if (typeof message.type !== 'string') {\n    return {valid: false, error: 'Message must have a type field'};\n  }\n\n  switch (message.type) {\n    case 'user_message':\n      return validateUserMessage(message);\n    case 'ping':\n      return validatePingMessage(message);\n    case 'list_sessions':\n      return {valid: true, data: {type: 'list_sessions'}};\n    default:\n      return {valid: false, error: `Unknown message type:
